### Psalm 120.

I call to the LORD in my distress, and He hears me.
<note>original: "HErr" – the use of all-caps for the name of the Lord is a standard feature in early German Bibles and devotional texts to denote the Tetragrammaton.</note>

### Psalm 145.

The LORD is near to all those who call upon Him, to all who call upon Him in earnest. He does what the God-fearing desire, He hears their crying out, and helps them.

### John 4.
<note>original: "Johan. 4"</note>

The time is coming, and is already now here, that the true worshipers will worship the Father in spirit and in truth; for the Father also wants to have such people who worship him in this way.

### Ephesians 6.
<note>original: "Ephes. 6"</note>

Pray always in every concern, with prayer and supplication in the spirit, and watch for this with all perseverance for all the saints.
<note>In this context, "saints" (original: *Heiligen*) refers to the general community of Christian believers rather than canonized figures.</note>

### 1 Thessalonians 5.
<note>original: "1 Thessal. 5"</note>

Pray without ceasing, be thankful in all things, for this is the will of GOD in JESUS Christ for you.

# Instruction,
## On how one should pray rightly.

How powerful, potent, and healing a thing—indeed, how like a lovely, sweet fragrance before GOD—a truly Christian, deeply fervent prayer of faith is, can be clearly understood from Holy Scripture, from the examples and fruits of prayers being answered, and also from the words and promises of our Lord Christ himself. For in the Gospel, he exhorts us to prayer many times and says: Ask, and it shall be given to you; seek, and you shall find; knock, and it shall be opened unto you, etc. <note>original: "2c." – a common abbreviation for "und so weiter" (etc.).</note> Matthew 7. For this reason, a person should accustom themselves to prayer with all earnestness and diligence. And because prayer is an uplifting of the soul <note>original: "Gemüths" – this term encompasses the mind, spirit, and emotional disposition of a person.</note> to GOD, and a heartfelt, loving conversation with the heavenly, kind King and Lord JESUS Christ, we should, when we wish to pray, cast all other things from our minds,

<summary>This page introduces the theological basis for prayer, describing it as a sweet fragrance to God and a direct conversation with Christ, while citing Matthew 7 as a divine mandate to pray.</summary>

<keywords>instruction, nature of prayer, Matthew 7, faith, conversation with God, Christian devotion, Gemüth</keywords>

<header>Instruction on how one should pray rightly.</header>

<margin>Psalm 145.</margin>
...gather all our thoughts, indeed, lift our whole heart to GOD and Christ, and conduct ourselves in no other way than as if the Lord were present before our eyes—just as He truly is present to all those who call upon Him, to all those who call upon Him in earnest. <note>A reference to Psalm 145:18: "The Lord is near to all who call on him, to all who call on him in truth."</note> We should lay before Him all our need, distress, sorrow, and concerns—above all, that we be delivered from sins—with fervent desires <note>original: "hitzigen Begierden" – literally "heated" or "burning" desires, emphasizing a passionate intensity in prayer.</note>; we should also, with faith, perceive and wait for His hearing, answer, comfort, help, and assistance in all that which is for our salvation.

<margin>Matthew 6, 7.</margin>
Behold, thus should we prepare ourselves for prayer, if we wish to pray rightly. But now, in times past, along with many other superstitions and errors, a very ruinous and grave abuse has taken root in Christian prayer, which unfortunately still persists today even among those who have recognized the truth to some degree: namely, that one makes prayer a cold, dead, external, and futile <note>original: "vergeben" – meaning in vain or useless.</note> work, by merely reciting the words without spirit and faith, babbling along <note>original: "hinplappert" – to prattle or chatter without thought; a reference to the "vain repetitions" of the Gentiles mentioned in Matthew 6:7.</note> with the mouth without heart, love, or earnestness.

<vocab>Prayer, Earnestness, Faith, Superstition, Error, Abuse, Christianity, Psalm 145, Matthew 6</vocab>

<keywords>earnestness, presence of God, fervent desire, spiritual abuse, rote prayer, sincerity, Psalm 145, Matthew 6</keywords>

### Instruction on how one should pray rightly.

...in such a way that one also perceives very little in the heart of the answer and the hearing of the Lord—indeed, of the gracious presents and gifts which God distributes to faithful <note>original: "rechtgläubigen" – meaning those who hold the correct or true faith.</note> pray-ers, even often in the midst of prayer. I will say nothing of the fact that many of them want to know nothing of such divine response, nor how the Lord Christ is accustomed to speak with His own in the Holy Spirit; instead, as soon as the words of the prayer, or the Our Father <note>The "Vater Unser" or Lord's Prayer.</note>, are finished, they run back to their old sinful ways without any devotion, without further attending to the powerful answer and hearing. Truly, with our praying, we may not unfairly be compared to the hypocrites of whom the Lord says, quoting the prophecy of Isaiah: "This people draws near to me with their mouth, and honors me with their lips, but their heart is far from me." (Matthew 15, Isaiah 29).

Just so, it has now unfortunately also fallen into abuse that we dispatch our Lord and God with our cold, weary, and lazy prayer (when it even happens that we pray at all) like a messenger in the morning <note>The author uses a vivid metaphor here: treating God like a servant who is given a quick set of instructions ("dispatched") so that the master can go about his day without further thought.</note>, and then throughout the entire day think of Him no more, much less of the fruit of prayer, which

### Instruction on how one should pray rightly.

is, the power, the blessing, and the **working** of **GOD** in our souls and consciences, as well as in outward things, we perceive very little.

Since this is indeed the case, how then may we be heard by God amidst such inattentiveness and sloth? And how should that for which we pray be heard in any way, since we pray without devotion, without attending to the words, and without understanding and faith? What wonder is it, then, if we wish to deal with God the Lord so carelessly and haughtily, without earnestness and the proper devotion of the heart? <note>As we deal with God, so He also deals with us, Psalm 18. original: "Psal. 18"</note> That He not only leaves us to remain and persist in **vices** and **sins**, but allows us to fall ever further into all kinds of error, presumption, and godlessness, leading to the greater punishment and pain of **hell**, and to our destruction.

Therefore, whichever person wishes to escape the coming evil, punishment, and judgment of **GOD**, and would gladly dwell with **Christ** in the kingdom of heaven, let him see to it above all things that he learns to pray rightly—yes, that he may exhort and call upon the Lord **Christ**, just as the disciples once did, that He—

### Instruction on how one should pray rightly.

...would teach him to pray rightly, and not only teach him to pray, but inwardly open and purify the ears of his heart, and so recollect his mind <note>original: "Gemüth dermassen einziehen" — a term for "recollection," the spiritual practice of pulling one's thoughts away from the external world to focus inward on God.</note> that he may also comfortingly hear the Lord's lovely answer in prayer.

To this we should then apply ourselves daily with all earnestness, giving no place, room, or stead to wandering thoughts during prayer. Instead, as soon as they try to overwhelm us, we should cast them out and fight earnestly against them, turning our heart only toward Christ, and through Christ to **GOD**. We should also take notice when the Spirit of the Lord graciously moves us to pause upon a certain word or petition and offers His help, or perhaps teaches, exhorts, or reveals something good within it (as **GOD** the Lord very often teaches His friends about the mysteries of His kingdom, even in the midst of prayer). We should not disturb ourselves during such a gracious gift and work of **GOD**, but rather keep a <term>Sela</term> <note>A Hebrew term found in the Psalms, traditionally used to mark a liturgical pause or a moment of reflection.</note>, or a stillness of spirit, and pay attention to what the Lord speaks through the Holy Spirit in our souls—yes, how the living Word of **GOD** spreads within our hearts.

<meta>catchword: and</meta>

# Instruction on how one should pray rightly.

<sig>A 5</sig>

listen with an eager spirit, through which we may also be unburdened of the works of Satan and darkness, as well as the evil desires of the flesh, and may become increasingly partakers of the benefit of Christ with true feeling in the Holy Spirit.

All this is often given and bestowed by the merciful **GOD** through Christ in the prayer of faith (just as it is also a powerful weapon against the evil spirit and against all Satanic seductions), for through it we receive power and might to fight chivalrously <note>original: "ritterlich" — the author uses the metaphor of a knight to describe the spiritual struggle of the believer.</note> against the sin that clings to us and against all enmity toward **GOD**, and to overcome the world with all its luxury <note>original: "Ueppigkeit" — can mean physical luxury, opulence, or spiritual pride/excess.</note> and the temptation of frivolity, as well as all other adversity, boldly and with a strong spirit. Thereupon the human heart becomes joyful in **GOD**, and the conscience is pacified and quieted—all through the working of the grace of Christ our Lord. In this grace, the faithful person also grows and increases daily in all righteousness, in all godliness <term>Gottseligkeit: a central Pietist concept meaning a life devoted to God that results in inner blessedness</term>, and upright truth, provided they remain and persevere steadfastly through faith in Christ.

# Instruction on how one should pray rightly.

This is also the fruit that always follows righteous prayer, not to mention that others for whom one prays also enjoy such blessings before **GOD** for their salvation. Therefore, let us cry out and pray to the Lord without ceasing, along with the dear disciples.

<header>Short Confession of Sins.</header>

Therefore, I will now begin to pray for this. O God, to You I lift up my eyes; my heart, soul, and everything within me shall praise Your holy name, Amen.

---

## A Short Confession of Sins.

We have sinned, O **LORD God**, with our fathers; <note>original: *mit unsern Vätern* — a reference to Psalm 106:6, acknowledging collective and ancestral human fallenness.</note> we have done wrong inwardly through desires, lusts, and thoughts; we have often committed vice and malice outwardly through words and deeds before You and before men: O You Father of all grace and mercy, a God of faith, of righteousness, of love, of hope, of patience, of comfort, of peace, and of all godliness; <note>original: *Gottseligkeit* — a central Pietist concept meaning a life of active, heartfelt devotion to God.</note> we poor, sinful people ought to pray to You, but how we are able to do so we do not know; indeed, we can neither do nor accomplish it of ourselves (without Your divine help, power, and assistance); give us Your Holy Spirit, who may teach us to pray in spirit and in truth, <note>A reference to John 4:24, "God is a Spirit: and they that worship him must worship him in spirit and in truth."</note> and forgive

<summary>This page contains a transitional prayer for spiritual focus followed by the beginning of a formal confession of sins, emphasizing that true prayer is only possible through the help of the Holy Spirit.</summary>

## A Short Confession of Sins.

We have sinned, O **Lord God**, along with our fathers; we have committed injustice inwardly through desires, lusts, and thoughts; we have often performed vice and malice outwardly through words and deeds before You and before men. O You Father of all grace and mercy, a **God** of faith, of righteousness, of love, of hope, of patience, of comfort, of peace, and of all godliness <note>original: *Gottseligkeit* — a central term in German devotional life, referring to a state of being "blessed in God" through active, sincere piety rather than just formal religious observance.</note>; we poor, sinful humans ought to pray to You, but how we are able to do so we do not know; indeed, we can neither do nor achieve it of our own selves (without Your divine help, power, and assistance); give us Your **Holy Spirit**, who teaches us to pray in spirit and in truth, and forgive us

<meta>catchword: us</meta>

<summary>This page introduces a formal confession of sins, emphasizing that humans are incapable of even praying correctly without the guidance of the Holy Spirit. It highlights the distinction between "inward" sins of thought and "outward" sins of deed.</summary>

For when I was nothing, You created me as a rational human being, poured a living soul into me, warmed me with Your breath, and formed me to receive Your image, so that I might also be further stirred <note>original: *gereizet* — in this historical context, to be prompted, moved, or incited by a holy impulse.</note> to Your praise. And so that I might be receptive in my soul to You Yourself, who are the highest and most loving Good, You have endowed me for this in many ways, raised me through Your providence, delivered me from various dangers to what is useful for me, and often guided me inwardly. ## Prayer on Christmas Night, to GOD the Father.

> Almighty, merciful **GOD**, heavenly Father, since You, out of bottomless mercy <note>original: *grundloser Barmherzigkeit* — an idiomatic way of describing mercy so deep it cannot be measured or "bottomed."</note> which You have held toward the human race, have let Your eternal, only Son become human <term>Incarnation: the theological belief that God took on human flesh in the person of Jesus.</term>, so that He might bring us poor lost people back, lead us to You, and through His holy birth, wipe away our sinful birth <note>This refers to the concept of "Original Sin," the inherited state of sinfulness which the speaker believes is cleansed by the purity of Christ's birth.</note>, so we pray to You from the heart: that You would, for the sake of His holy birth and incarnation, graciously forgive us everything with which we—according to our old sinful birth and nature—have sinned against Your divine majesty in this past year; and that You would grant us, through that same Son of Yours, **JESUS** Christ, a good and blessed New Year, with an increase of Your grace, Amen.

# Prayer on Christmas Night, to GOD the Son.

> Lord Jesus Christ, You newly born King of Grace, we give You thanks that on this night, with a new light of Your holy birth and incarnation <term>Incarnation: The theological concept of God taking on human form and flesh.</term>, You have enlightened the world, for the comfort, salvation, and eternal blessedness of us poor humans who sit in the shadow and darkness of death. Praised be You, Lord Jesus Christ, that You have so graciously protected and shielded us throughout this past year; that You have also sustained us and so generously provided for us with all necessities <note>original: *Nothdurft* — referring to the basic physical and spiritual requirements for life.</note> pertaining to soul and body, and that You increasingly reveal the knowledge of Yourself. Ah, we pray to You for the sake of Your lovely, benevolent <note>original: *leutseligen* — an archaic term meaning kind, approachable, or loving toward humanity.</note> childhood and birth: that You would also stand by us in this following year in all needs and anxieties, and that You would cleanse us today through Your Spirit from all our sins, and give us new birth in You for the inheritance of Your kingdom and eternal life; and also make us strong and bold <note>original: *keck* — meaning brave or spirited in one's faith.</note>, so that we may [proclaim/praise] Your power, glory, and splendor, and also Your divine truth ever [longer...] <note>The text cuts off here; the catchword "län," suggests the next page begins with "länger," likely completing the phrase "ever longer and more."</note>

# The "Te Deum Laudamus" <note>original: *Das Te Deum laudamus* — "Thee, O God, we praise." One of the most famous and ancient hymns of the Christian church, traditionally sung on occasions of great joy or thanksgiving.</note>

<meta>catchword: You</meta>

O God, we praise You, we acknowledge You to be the Lord.
The whole earth praises You, the eternal Father.
To You all angels cry aloud, the heavens, and all the heavenly powers.
To You the Cherubim <term>Cherubim: A high order of angels often associated with the presence and majesty of God.</term> and Seraphim <term>Seraphim: Celestial beings described as having six wings, who cry out God's holiness in a perpetual song.</term> sing with unceasing voice:
Holy, holy, holy, Lord God Sabaoth <term>Sabaoth: A Hebrew title meaning "of Hosts" or "of Armies," referring to God's sovereignty over all celestial and earthly forces.</term>.
Heaven and earth are full of the majesty of Your glory.
The glorious assembly of all Your messengers <note>original: *Boten* — In the traditional Latin version, this refers specifically to the "glorious company of the Apostles."</note> honors You as the one true God.
Likewise, the praiseworthy number of Your prophets does so at all times.
The whole army of holy martyrs praises You with high witness.
The holy Church acknowledges You throughout the entire circle of the earth.
The Father of an immeasurable, high majesty.
And Your honorable, true, and only Son Jesus Christ; they hold Him, with You, as true God and Lord.
Also the Holy Spirit, the true Comforter.
O Christ, the King of Glory.
You are the eternal Son of the Father.

# The "Te Deum Laudamus" <note>This ancient hymn is traditionally divided into three parts: praise to the Trinity, praise to Christ the Redeemer, and a series of petitions for mercy.</note>

You did not disdain the virgin’s womb when You assumed humanity <note>original: *Menschheit* — Referring to the Incarnation, the belief that God became human in the form of Jesus.</note> to redeem mankind.

And after You overcame the sting of death, You opened the kingdom of heaven to all believers.

Now You sit at the right hand of God in the glory of Your Father.

And You are believed to be the judge who is to come.

Therefore we pray You, may You come to the aid of Your servants and ministers, whom You have redeemed with Your most holy and precious blood.

Grant that they may partake in eternal glory along with Your saints.

O Lord, save Your people and bless Your inheritance <note>original: *Erbtheil* — A biblical term referring to the people of God as His chosen possession.</note>. Govern them and exalt them for all eternity.

Every day we will praise You and give You thanks.

And we will praise Your name from eternity to eternity.

Ah Lord, let us entreat You <note>original: *laß dichs erbitten* — A humble request for God to be moved by prayer.</note> that You would keep us from sin this day.

Have mercy upon us, Lord, have mercy upon us.

And let Your mercy be over us, as we place our hope in You.

## Remembrance during the Passion of Christ.

The suffering <term>Passion: The "Passion" refers to the physical and spiritual suffering of Jesus Christ leading up to and during His crucifixion.</term> of Christ, when rightly understood and contemplated in faith, is a certain healing medicine for all kinds of sickness of the body and the soul; therefore, in every concern, tribulation, and adversity, as well as in all kinds of temptation from sin and the evil spirit, a person should quickly run with the thoughts of their heart to the cross of Christ and rightly contemplate His holy suffering. 

Indeed, one should diligently perceive with a believing heart who it is that suffered, how and why He suffered, so that they may fruitfully internalize <note>original: *einbilde* — in early modern German, this meant "to form an image within oneself" or "to imprint on the heart," rather than "to imagine" in the modern sense of fantasy.</note> for themselves the entire innocent suffering, death, and life of Christ. Everything adverse that happens to a person’s body and soul should be immersed into this contemplation, as if into a counterpart <note>original: *Gegenwurf* — likely meaning a spiritual anchor or a point of comparison where one's own small trials are weighed against the magnitude of Christ's sacrifice.</note>, to be considered and viewed through that lens.

There, the person in Christ, through His grace (which to all poor

<meta>catchword: Sinners (Sün)</meta>

<summary>This page serves as an introduction to a devotional meditation on the Passion of Christ, presenting His suffering as a universal spiritual "medicine" for all human trials. It instructs the reader to internalize the image of the crucifixion to find comfort and perspective for their own hardships.</summary>

<keywords>Passion of Christ, meditation, spiritual medicine, suffering, faith, internalizing Christ, grace</keywords>

<header>Prayer for the Entry of Christ, etc.</header>

<note>original: "Sündern, mit seinem bittern Leiden ist erworben"</note> sinners, earned through His bitter suffering) find all kinds of comfort, help, benefit, and support; there they will also find a noble exchange <note>This refers to the "Great Exchange" (fröhliche Wechsel), a theological concept where Christ takes on human sin and gives the believer His righteousness.</note> of virtue for their sinful nature and vice, along with a whole new godly life, and from Christ, the fountain of salvation, be able to draw true piety, forgiveness of sins, indeed all kinds of grace, peace, joy, and blessedness through faith.

To which end the following prayers, as well as the true knowledge of Christ, will be very serviceable, good, and beneficial; let these be diligently commended to you, O God-fearing person who desires eternal salvation, to pray devoutly along with the contemplation of the suffering of Christ, with a heart lifted up to God, in right understanding and faith. Amen.

# Prayer.

Lord **JESUS** Christ, you true light that shines eternally and can never be extinguished, you who have commanded us to watch and pray at all times so that we do not fall into temptation, since the spirit is willing but the poor flesh is very weak; I pray you, that you would drive away the deep sleep—indeed, the wicked <term>lethargy|Schlafsucht: literally "sleep-sickness," used here to describe a state of spiritual indifference or laziness</term> and sloth—of my soul, so that I may be fully awake against the mischievous evil spirits (who never sleep in their quest for our ruin), and that I may seek and call upon you with fervent prayer from the heart, so that (through the assistance of your grace) I may not be overcome by them, nor found in obedience to their wickedness, Amen.

## Prayer.

O Lord JESUS Christ, who was esteemed so lowly by one of your twelve disciples—those who were dearest to you—and was sold for <term>thirty pennies|dreysig Pfennig: while the Bible specifies thirty pieces of silver, the author uses the currency of their own time to make the betrayal feel more immediate and relatable to the reader</term>, betrayed, and delivered into the hands of your enemies for the sake of our salvation; you who did not even wish to refuse the godless Judas the kiss with which he betrayed you: grant me that I may always hold you dear and worthy in my heart, that I may never exchange or hand you over for any earthly thing, nor in any way withdraw from you to my own ruin, but rather stay and persevere with you until the end and eternally for the preservation of my soul. Grant me also that I may always repay all my enemies with love and good for their evil and wrongdoing, according to your example. Amen.

## Prayer.

O Lord JESUS Christ, you who stood before Pilate and your false accusers—the wicked people who burdened you with various blasphemies <note>original: "den bösen Juden." The text reflects the common anti-Judaic sentiment of early modern devotional literature, which often cast the Jewish leadership as the primary antagonists in the Passion narrative.</note>—and answered not a word, but like a lamb before the shearer <note>A reference to the messianic prophecy in Isaiah 53:7: "as a sheep before her shearers is dumb, so he openeth not his mouth."</note>, did not wish to open your mouth, nor be found rebellious or defiant in any matter; oh, grant me also that I may patiently overcome all insulting speech, persecution, and the injustice of my adversaries through silence, and that I may do, say, or begin nothing with a stirred or angry spirit, Amen.

### 29. Then Pilate took JESUS and scourged Him.

# Prayer.

LORD JESUS Christ, you who allowed your holy limbs to be scourged and wounded quite mercilessly with harsh blows for the sake of our salvation, so that by your wounds you might heal <note>original: "arzeneyest," meaning to treat with medicine or heal</note> our wounded soul, and by your stripes <note>original: "Striemen," referring to the welts or marks left by a whip</note> take away our guilt; I pray you, deliver me from the punishment of your eternal wrath, which I have well deserved since my youth, and let me patiently accept the scourge of your chastisement <note>original: "Züchtigung," referring to divine discipline or correction</note> here on earth, Amen.

## Prayer.

> O Lord JESUS Christ, you who before your crucifixion were stripped of your garments and, for the sake of our salvation, were willing to be stretched naked upon the wood [of the cross], so that through your extreme poverty—in which you chose not only to be born, to live, and to walk, but also to die—you might pay for our harmful <term>immoderation: original "Unmäßigkeit," referring to a lack of self-control or spiritual excess</term> and make our spiritual poverty rich; I pray you, strip off my <term>old man: a biblical reference to the sinful human nature before spiritual renewal</term> and clothe me with a new one, created in your likeness in the righteousness and holiness of truth. May I follow you—my Lord JESUS, naked upon the cross, the <term>author and finisher of our faith: a reference to Hebrews 12:2</term>—having been stripped of all vices, Amen.

# Prayer.

> Lord JESUS Christ, you who are the sole peacemaker of all things in heaven and on earth, who for the sake of our salvation and that of all mankind were willing to hang upon the shameful wood (of the cross) in a high place between heaven and earth, so that you might <term>draw all things to yourself: a reference to John 12:32, where Jesus speaks of his death attracting all people to him</term>; oh dear Lord, I pray you, that you would lift up my miserable flesh—corrupted by sin, still crawling upon the earth, and of an earthly mind—into the victory of your cross. And since it is still full of impure, sinful desires, I pray that you would draw it up into your heavenly essence as much as is ever possible, so that I may despise everything that is earthly, everything that is fleshly down here and contrary to you. Grant that I love nothing besides you, and hold nothing to be high or wise except you alone, the crucified, glorified, and exalted King of Heaven. Let my love be directed toward you alone; let me desire you alone, look to you alone, and think, meditate, and speak of you. Grant that I may find all my delight and joy in you, and that my soul desires to be comforted in nothing except in you alone, my Lord JESUS, Amen.

## Prayer.

> Lord **Jesus Christ**, you who throughout your entire life—which was full of the cross, full of hardship, tribulation, anxiety, and suffering—remained set apart from all bodily pleasure; you who also, while on the Cross, were given vinegar and gall to drink to atone for our <term>Lustfraß: a strong term for gluttonous desire or an insatiable appetite for worldly pleasures</term>; I pray to you: cleanse my mouth, which is unfortunately badly stained, corrupted, and poisoned by the sweet venom of lust. Let me taste your sweetness, so that those things which have foolishly amused my earthly self begin to become bitter to me. O Lord **Jesus**, graciously grant that from now on it may be much dearer to me to suffer disgrace and persecution with you here for the sake of your truth, than to live in the temporal lust of sin. Preserve me so that no bitterness, gall, or injustice may proceed from my mouth or heart, Amen.

## Prayer.

# Prayer.

**Lord Jesus Christ**, in whose death the sun of the world withdrew its light, the curtain of the temple was torn, the earth shook, the rocks split, and the dead were raised <note>These are the miraculous signs recorded in the Gospels as occurring the moment Jesus died.</note>; I ask you most earnestly that the sun of your life-giving grace may never leave poor me. Instead, through the influence of your brilliance, drive away the darkness, mist, and gloom of the old, corrupted earth of my heart. Take away all hypocrisy, every covering and curtain, so that I may rightly know myself and come to a blessed repentance. O Lord, circumcise my heart <note>A biblical metaphor for spiritual purification and devotion to God.</note>; yes, take away the hardened heart of stone and give me in its place a soft heart of flesh <note>A reference to Ezekiel 36:26, promising a transformation from stubbornness to spiritual sensitivity.</note> in which you may write your will. Do this so that this dead corpse, through your healing death, may rise into the newness of life and finally be made entirely like the body of your glory, Amen.

# Prayer.

> **Lord Jesus Christ**, from whose body—which died and was made alive again—a most blessed spring-fountain has gently flowed forth, which grants us water for purification and blood for the giving of life <term>Lebendigmachung: the act of restoring to life or quickening</term>; namely, that through the one we might be made alive from death, and through the other—the water of your grace—we might be washed and purified from our impurity in the baptism of the Holy Spirit; I pray you, wound my heart with the beam of your love: Oh, have mercy on me, who by nature am entirely stained, devastated, and covered with the filth of sin; and grant to me that I may partake in the mystery of the blood and water which flows from your holy side into all believing hearts, for the washing away of my sins, and that I may become clean, pure, and worthy for the reception of the new eternal Testament <note>The "Testament" here refers to the New Covenant established by Christ's death</note>, Amen.

## Prayer.

> **Lord** Jesus Christ, though your entire life was full of gentleness, humility, patience, and kindness, yet you allowed your death to be so solemn, strong, and powerful that those who came to this spectacle <note>The term "spectacle" here refers to the public nature of the execution, which drew a large crowd of onlookers.</note>, when they saw what occurred, were violently startled, turned inward, and beat their breasts; O Lord my God, grant me that I may be not only a spectator, but also a follower of your humility, patience, and your cross; likewise, may I hold your exaltation high and glorious, so that I may never cease from the contemplation of your suffering without a broken, repentant heart (which you, God, do not despise <note>A reference to Psalm 51:17: "A broken and a contrite heart, O God, thou wilt not despise."</note>) and without special benefit and fruit, Amen.

## Prayer.

## A Prayer and Contemplation of Christ’s Suffering, to GOD the Father, for the Forgiveness of Sins.

> I call upon you, O my **GOD**, heavenly Father, I call upon you today from the bottom of my heart, as much as it is possible for me, a poor sinner, to do, and I ask you, that for the sake of the unspeakable love of your Son **JESUS Christ** (through which he gave himself into bitter death for my sins), you would be gracious and merciful to me; lead my soul out of the prison of death, that it may freshly and joyfully praise and confess your holy name; free me from the bonds of sins, fear, and great weakness, so that I may be placed into the freedom and righteousness of the children of **GOD**, and be richly endowed with your <term>princely Spirit</term> <note>original: "fürstlichen Geiste." This term (Spiritus Principalis) refers to a noble, governing, or steadfast spirit, often associated with the Holy Spirit's role in guiding the believer with strength and dignity.</note>, Amen.
> 
> Ah, that I today and at all times [might enjoy] the comforting intercession of **JESUS Christ** my **Lord** <note>The text here breaks off, as this is the end of the page. The phrase likely continues on the following page to describe the benefits of Christ's mediation.</note>

## Narrative of the Benefits of Christ.

O how much good has your Son, my Lord Jesus Christ, often shown me from my youth onward, even in the midst of my evil, sinful life! For this I have been so very little thankful to you and to him; yet he has not rejected me, nor has he ever forsaken me. In times of need he has stood by me; from many dangers he has rescued me; when I strayed, he brought me back; when I sinned, he disciplined <note>original: "züchtigte." In this context, it refers to divine chastening or corrective discipline intended to lead a person back to a righteous path.</note> me; when I was wounded, he healed me; when I was in want, he provided for me; out of much darkness he led me; the truth he taught me.

<header>Prayer to Christ in Times of Trouble.</header>

# Prayer to Christ in Times of Trouble.

> O **Lord Jesus Christ**, you mighty King of Grace, have mercy upon your poor, weak <note>The term "creature" (German: *Geschöpfe*) emphasizes the absolute dependence of the human being on their Creator.</note> creature; let it not perish in your judgment, but rather be refreshed in the cool dew of your grace, and enjoy your <term>exaltation<note>Refers to the theological concept of Christ’s "Stufe der Erhöhung," his triumph over death and his return to a state of divine glory at the right hand of God.</note></term> at the right hand of the Majesty of **GOD**. Let me become a partaker of your glory, power, and essence, so that I may feed, nourish, and satisfy myself therewith, and henceforth turn away from all earthly things and <note>Original: "männlich," meaning to resist with spiritual courage and steadfastness.</note> manfully renounce all desires of the flesh. Grant that I may henceforth partake in such exaltation of yours—indeed, being born anew and again from above through you—and bring forth fruits that are pleasing and acceptable to you and your Father. Let yourself be entreated for this today, you Giver of all good things, you Dispenser of all heavenly gifts, you Founder of peace, of everlasting joy, righteousness, and salvation. Do not forsake me, poor and abandoned as I am; do not turn away from me, a needy and sorrowful soul, for you are my true, living **God**. You are my holy Father, a Father of eternity; you are my kind **Lord**, my joyful King, my faithful good Shepherd, and also my only Master in teaching.

<vocab>Prayer, Christ, Grace, Exaltation, Majesty, Rebirth, Peace, Righteousness, Salvation</vocab>

<summary>This page contains a passionate prayer for spiritual rebirth, asking Christ to share his divine glory and "exaltation" with the believer to overcome earthly desires. It concludes with a list of divine titles emphasizing Christ's roles as Father, Shepherd, and Teacher.</summary>

## Lift up your hearts and let us pray.

O **Lord God**, heavenly Father, we poor carnal humans, imprisoned in the law of sin <note>original: 'Sünden Gesetz'—a Pauline concept referring to the innate human tendency toward sin that conflicts with God's will</note>, come before you today with faithful confidence in your grace and mercy. We confess all our sins to you, humbly praying—if we are at all capable of asking for anything—that you would desire to renew our hearts and endow us with your Holy Spirit. We ask that through Him, you would pour out your divine love within us, so that we may become more righteous, merciful, gentle, ever more patient, and more pious from day to day. May we also become increasingly more understanding and joyful <note>original: 'lustiger'—in this period, the word meant 'joyful,' 'cheerful,' or 'eager' in a spiritual sense, rather than 'funny'</note> in spiritual matters that advance your kingdom, so that we may love and acknowledge you and your Son **Jesus Christ** from the heart, in which we have eternal life. We commend ourselves to you in all our actions, conduct, and ways <term>Handel und Wandel: an idiomatic expression referring to one's entire manner of living and social interactions</term>, in words, works, and thoughts. Guide and promote everything within us according to your holy will, and to your divine praise and glory, so that your name alone may be lauded and praised in all things. Come to the aid of our great-

# Regarding Grace for Sin

...now, out of sorrow and suffering, did not know where they should turn.

> Dear **Lord Jesus**, let me also today and every day be such a **Peter** <note>original: "Petrum"</note>, I who have, unfortunately, often denied you from my youth onwards through my evil, sinful life, whereby I have conducted myself unworthily in your service, so that I have often not earnestly resisted the attractions of the flesh; forgive me for this as you did **Peter**, my **Lord Christ**, and teach me henceforth to walk cautiously before your divine eyes; let me indeed be like that same **Peter**, my **Lord Jesus Christ**. Let those for whom I pray also be such people to whom you would reveal your glorious and joy-filled resurrection; also let us feel with joy that you have now indeed risen in our hearts.

> And though we do not yet see you so soon in your actual person, may you nevertheless go before us into **Galilee** <note>The author uses Galilee here as a metaphor for the spiritual journey or the transition from earthly life to the divine presence, referencing the biblical account where the risen Christ tells the disciples he will go before them to Galilee (Matthew 28:7).</note>, that is, that you would show us the way home into the **Fatherland** <note>In this context, "Fatherland" (Vaterland) refers to the heavenly home or the kingdom of God, a common trope in early modern devotional literature.</note>, to you into eternal life; that we may follow you in all virtuous words, thoughts, senses, with the will and good works, so that we may then truly see you, yes, inwardly and outwardly recog[nize]... <note>The text ends mid-word with the catchword "erken," which would complete as "erkennen" (to recognize or know) on the following page.</note>

<summary>This page contains a deeply personal prayer where the speaker identifies with the Apostle Peter’s denial of Christ, seeking similar forgiveness and a spiritual "resurrection" within their own heart. The author uses the biblical journey to Galilee as a metaphor for the soul's path toward eternal life in the "Fatherland" of heaven.</summary>

## A Confession of Sins and a Plea for Mercy.

Have mercy, O Lord God, have mercy on me, a poor, needy sinner, who often sins, transgresses, and acts unjustly before you, yet quite justly <note>original: "billig" — in this archaic context, it means "rightly" or "deservedly" rather than "inexpensive."</note> endure your punishment and chastisement <term>chastisement: corrective discipline intended to lead a person back to a righteous path.</term>; for when I consider the evil I have done from my youth onward, and how often I have sinned against you, compared to how you punish me and give me the bread of sorrow to eat, there is still no comparison; it is nothing but pure grace and goodness with you, in all your works. 

Ah Lord God, how righteous you are, how upright are all your judgments and ways; you are ever pious and good, and there is never any malice to be found in you; neither is there any wickedness nor deceit, nor any vengefulness to be found in you: For all poor sinners who cry out to you when they fall, and simply rise again in your grace with full intent <note>original: "Fürsatz" — a firm resolution or purposeful intent to change one's life.</note> and convert themselves, those you take

# Prayer for the Authorities.

Almighty God, you who desire that all social ranks <note>original: "Stände"; referring to the various classes or "estates" (nobility, clergy, commons) of society.</note> and orders of mankind should be saved; we pray to you for the sake of your unfathomable <note>original: "grundlosen"; literally "groundless," meaning a mercy so deep it has no bottom.</note> mercy, that you would look upon all authorities ordained according to your will, and grant them the knowledge of your dear Son **Jesus Christ**, who is the divine wisdom, to whom all power in heaven and on earth belongs, and bestow upon them that they may lead a Christian, blessed, and peaceful reign <note>original: "Regiment"; refers to the administration or government of the land.</note> in him, so that all quarreling, strife, and bloodshed may be stilled, and you may be served in a quiet spirit, in faith, and in true love. Give also to all subjects <note>original: "Unterthanen"; those living under the jurisdiction of the authorities.</note> that they may be found blameless in true love toward their authorities. Grant to all who call upon you in truth that they may increase in your holy knowledge, doing the good and forsaking the evil. Give to all troubled hearts rest and comfort; uphold in your power all those who suffer and have affliction, especially for the sake of your truth; and to those who do not yet know of you, grant that they may recognize you and convert to you, whereby they [may] have <note>The text ends mid-word with "ha-"; the catchword "ben" from the metadata indicates the word is "haben" (to have).</note> eternal life—

# Prayer for Mercy.

O you kind, merciful **GOD**,
heavenly Father, since in
these final, perilous times it appears
as though you have forgotten us and the
demonstration of your graces; as if you, on
account of our great ingratitude and heavy
sins, have entirely abandoned us—as is
shown all too clearly, alas, by the 
manifold division of hearts <note>A reference to social and religious discord common during the era this was written.</note>, the
common godless way of life, also the
ignorant zeal <note>Original: "unverständiger Eifer." This refers to misguided religious fervor or fanaticism that lacks true spiritual understanding.</note> of many people, and the
fruits and works of our unrepentant lives;
of all of which we confess ourselves
guilty before your divine eyes.
Therefore we come to you as poor, indebted
people, and remind you of
your great, inexpressible mercy,
with which heaven and earth are full, with
the most humble petition—because your
name is also called Mercy, and **Christ JESUS**
is your beloved Son—that you would
allow this, your name and your mercy,
to appear to us and to
remember our sins no more; that you
would spare our ingratitude and
grant us your goodness,
which you have graciously promised to all
who call upon you from the heart.
For, though you [hide] yourself for a time <note>The sentence continues onto the next page.</note>,

## A Blessing in Christ.

Now, all of you beloved, surrender yourselves to the Lord Jesus in your hearts with true faith; learn to recognize your shortcomings, you should repent of and weep for your sins, and then joyfully direct your heart and conscience toward the **Eradicator** <term>Eradicator (original: "Austilger"); one who completely wipes out or blots out a debt or stain, referring here to Christ removing the guilt of sin.</term> and Forgiver of sin, Jesus Christ, and He will forgive you all your sins. Learn to recognize Him ever more in His **Office of Grace** <term>Office of Grace (original: "Gnaden-Amt"); the theological role of Christ in which He offers mercy and reconciliation to humanity.</term>, which brings the forgiveness of sins with it. Love Him from the heart and walk in the obedience of true Christian faith, so that you avoid sin and pursue what is good and **sanctification** <term>sanctification (original: "Heiligung"); the process of being made holy or being set apart for God's service through the work of the Holy Spirit.</term> ever more diligently; then the Lord Jesus will dwell in your hearts, and you will remain with Him in His Father's kingdom forever. May God grant this, Amen.

Do not be ashamed to speak of the Lord, to sing, and to praise his name; for the Lord is great and highly to be praised, and his greatness is beyond expression; gracious and merciful is our God, he is slow to anger, and is of great kindness; the Lord is friendly to everyone, and his mercy is over all his works. # Prayer to the Holy Spirit.

bestow upon us the love of the Father, the merit of the Son, in you and through your sanctification <term>Heiligung</term>, you who are the Spirit of the power of heaven, of divine life, of true love, holiness, and unity.

Mortify and burn our old, cold hearts with your divine fire, so that all self-seeking <note>original: "Eigengesuch"; refers to the pursuit of one's own interests or ego rather than God's will</note>, all fleshly love for creatures <term>Creaturen</term> <note>In this period, "creatures" refers to any created thing—people, objects, or earthly pleasures—as opposed to the Creator</note> may fall away; so that all useless worries, all human cunning and cleverness may give way to your mastery <term>Meisterschaft</term>; so that we may serve you without worry and with a quiet heart, and rightly grasp true Christian simplicity <term>Einfalt</term>, to the end that our heart may love, praise, and serve **GOD** alone and become His holy temple; sanctify us more and more. Give us one mind, a common counsel, one heart, courage, and soul directed toward **GOD** and Christ. Drive out all evil thoughts, all suspicion, all self-will <note>original: "Eigenheit"; here meaning stubbornness or the insistence on one's own particular ways over communal or divine harmony</note>, all satanic desires, and help us in our weakness in every way, so that we may rightly discern all that you reveal for our benefit, judge everything well, recognize everything rightly, and use it for your praise; and guard us so that we in no way mix, stain, or in any manner corrupt your gifts with the desires of our flesh.

# A Psalm of Blessing.

Our help comes from the Lord, who
has made heaven and earth.

May the Lord hear you in the time of trouble;
may the name of the God of Jacob protect you.

May he send you help from the sanctuary, and
strengthen you from Zion.

May he give you what you have in mind, and
fulfill all your plans <term>Anschläge</term> <note>Original: "Anschläge"; in this context, it refers to one's purposes, designs, or the "devices" of the heart</note>.

He will not let your feet slip,
and he who guards you does not sleep.

The Lord guard you from all evil,
may he guard your soul.

The Lord guard your coming in and
your going out, from now until eternity,
Amen.

May the Lord Jesus, the true Son
of God, who gathers us in his name,
who also grants us strength so that
we may call upon God in all our troubles,
grant us that we may withstand all spiritual trials <term>Anfechtungen</term> <note>Original: "Anfechtungen"; a key term in Lutheran theology referring to intense spiritual crises, temptations, or the feeling of being abandoned by God</note>,
and let us poor, weak, and forsaken ones be
commended to him today,
at all times and into eternity,
Amen.

# Another Prayer to the Holy Spirit.

We poor, forsaken, and wretched children, who are subject to much tribulation, anguish, and danger both inwardly and outwardly on account of our sins, call upon you today, O you Holy Spirit, you Spirit of the heavenly Father and of our Lord Jesus Christ; come to us, and bring down to us from above a bright radiance of your divine light into our dark and gloomy hearts, so that in your light we may see the eternal light and rightly recognize Jesus Christ.

Come, you Father of wretched orphans <note>The term "orphans" here refers to the spiritual state of feeling abandoned or fatherless without God's presence</note>, you gentle distributor of all good gifts, come, you purifier of all unclean hearts, and exercise your office in us; sanctify us and set to the work for which Christ received and sent you from the Father, among us poor ones who desire you from the heart. Even though we (alas) [deserve] all punishment because of our unrepentant life and the great ingratitude toward you, as well as our fleshly <note>The text ends mid-word with "ver-", likely meaning "verdienet" (deserved) or "verschuldet" (guilt/indebtedness), which would continue on the next page.</note>

<header>Prayer to the Lord Christ, etc.</header>

be completely extinguished. Let us not
be driven into the blindness of ignorance in divine
matters, nor into idolatry <note>original: "Abgötterey"; the worship of false idols or putting earthly things before God</note> and error
in any way, nor be led away from you (you
eternal Light) into damnable darkness;
but preserve us in
your truth, and increase it for us with
a new light for your glory.
Give peace, dear Lord, grant peace,
for the sake of the eternal peace that you
earned for us with your precious blood on the Cross.
Also grant graciously that we
henceforth may use all your gifts
with more gratitude and fruitfulness, unto salvation <term>seliglich: a term describing actions or states that lead toward or pertain to eternal heavenly bliss</term>.
That we may acknowledge you in all things as our Lord
and God without hypocrisy, and
rightly know you from the heart;
also, with the obedience of our lives, demonstrate to you and your Father
all submissiveness. Convert
us (Jesus Christ) and make us blessed,
strive and fight for us,
comfort and strengthen us in all danger, for the sake of your death,
also for the sake of your glory, name, and
majesty, as we then know
that all things stand in your hand.
Therefore you, as a Lord of Heaven and the
Earth, are well able to do all such things,
as we also believe: that you—as much as is
salutary for us—will, upon our pleading prayer and

<meta>catchword: An-</meta>

<summary>This page concludes a petition for spiritual enlightenment and peace, asking Christ to preserve the believer from spiritual blindness and hypocrisy. It emphasizes Christ's sovereignty as Lord of Heaven and Earth who has the power to grant all requests for the soul's benefit.</summary>
<keywords>prayer, Jesus Christ, spiritual light, idolatry, peace, blood of Christ, gratitude, obedience, sovereignty, salvation</keywords>
